X. Chen et al., "SiWB: A 28-nm 800-MHz 4.2-to-14.2-Gb/s/W Configurable Multi-Core Architecture for White-Box Block Cipher With Area-Efficient Random Linear Transformation and Load-Aware Inter-Core Scheduling," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3649035.
Abstract: White-box cryptography (WBC) seeks to protect secret keys (SKs) even under the white-box security model that features adversaries having full control of the execution environment. Due to the ever-growing demand for content protection under security-critical scenarios, the recent progress on WBC has been nothing short of spectacular. However, the security-prioritized strategy also brings massive computation overhead to the current dedicated white-box block cipher (DWBC), seriously hindering its broad application. Compared to traditional software-only solutions, this work proposes a highly efficient domain-specific and silicon-proven processor (named SiWB) to improve the throughput (energy efficiency) by hundreds (several orders) of magnitude. First, not only a standalone crypto-core in prior work, we develop a complete and robust system that integrates multiple hardware cores for accelerating DWBC, user-defined high-speed interface (UPIF) for improving data transmission speed and other synergetic components. Second, to enhance throughput while maintaining energy efficiency, we devise a multi-core architecture based on inter-core load-aware scheduling, ultimately achieving 11.4-Gb/s peak throughput and 14.2-Gb/s/W energy efficiency. Third, a configurable and area-efficient datapath supporting multi-size random linear transformation (LT) and non-LT (NLT) is designed to reduce resource redundancy by means of algorithm-hardware co-optimization. To our best knowledge, this is the first 28-nm silicon-proven hardware accelerator for white-box block ciphers under black-box mode, which occupies 1.8-mm2 area and achieves 800-MHz peak frequency at 0.9 V. It supports several white-box block ciphers including SPNbox-8/16/24/32, Yoroi-16/32, and white-box addition/rotation/XOR white-box addition/rotation/XOR (WARX), delivering  $389{\times }$  speedup on average versus software programs optimized with advanced encryption standard new instructions (AES-NIs) on a 14-core 3.4-GHz CPU. It enables WBC to be a viable solution for applications with stringent throughput requirements, such as streaming media commonly demanding several Gb/s.
keywords: {Glass box;Ciphers;Closed box;Throughput;Encryption;Energy efficiency;Computer architecture;Integrated circuit modeling;Hardware acceleration;Streaming media;Algorithm-hardware co-design;multi-core architecture;random linear transformation (LT);white-box block cipher},

D. Cho, B. Park, H. -S. Choi, M. -J. Lee and Y. Chae, "A SPAD Flash LiDAR Sensor With Push-Pull Analog Counter Achieving a 0.12% Depth Precision at a 70 m Depth Range," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2026.3655000.
Abstract: This article presents a  $64 {\times } 64$  SPAD flash LiDAR sensor that achieves a depth precision of 0.12% across a 70 m depth range. The sensor is built on a time-gated single-photon counting (TGSPC) architecture that integrates a back-side illuminated (BSI) single-photon avalanche diode (SPAD), a pulse-shaping circuit, a push-pull analog pulse counter, and column-parallel single-slope (SS) analog-to-digital converters (ADCs). A push-pull analog counter has been integrated into the pixel to significantly enhance the sensor’s ability to prevent saturation under high background light (BGL) conditions. Fabricated using a 110 nm BSI CMOS image sensor process, the sensor features a compact pixel pitch of  $32~{\mu }$ m and a high fill factor of 33%. For 3-D depth sensing, it operates at 30 fps with a power consumption of 29.1 mW. It achieves a high photon detection probability (PDP) of 14.3% at 850 nm, enabling a depth precision of 8.4 cm at a depth range of 70 m, corresponding to the depth precision of 0.12%. These results are achieved through the use of a compact SPAD front-end circuit, a novel push-pull analog counter, and an optimized SPAD structure.

D. -Y. Lim et al., "Dual-Mode Inverter-Based Write Termination Scheme for Energy- and Area-Efficient Write Operation in 28-nm 1T1MTJ STT-MRAM," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2026.3654895.
Abstract: Spin-transfer torque magnetic random access memory (STT-MRAM) is a promising non-volatile memory, but its write operation requires a large static current, leading to high write power. Resistance-monitoring write termination (RM-WT) schemes were introduced to reduce write power, but they suffer from several limitations: 1) the sensing circuit (SC) used in previous schemes has limited effective input voltage, degrading the robustness of WT; 2) the prior schemes cause severe area overhead due to the additional WT circuits besides sense amplifier (SA); and 3) the previous schemes cannot support write 0 termination, limiting write power reduction. This article proposes a dual-mode inverter-based write-termination (DMI-WT) circuit that resolves these limitations. In the proposed scheme, a single-ended inverter-based SC (INV-SC) is used, which doubles the effective input voltage without area overhead. In addition, self-referenced offset cancel (OC) is used, which enables robust WT and SA operation. DMI-WT enables WT operation for both write 0 and write 1 operation while simultaneously serving as an SA, thereby reducing the write power and removing the area overhead caused by WT circuits. A prototype chip fabricated in 28-nm CMOS technology with a 256-kb STT-MRAM array shows that DMI-WT achieves up to  $6.39{\times }$  area overhead reduction,  $7.5{\times }$  lower write power per bit at a 20-ns write access time,  $1.27{\times }$  faster read access time, and  $1.41{\times }$  lower read power compared with previous WT circuits using STT-MRAM.

J. -H. Lee, S. -J. Yang, M. -J. Lee and W. -Y. Choi, "25-Gb/s 850-nm Monolithic Optical Receiver With a Si APD Bias Controller Using Histogram Monitoring," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2026.3654962.
Abstract: We demonstrate a 25-Gb/s 850-nm monolithic optical receiver containing a Si avalanche photodetector (APD) realized in standard 28-nm complementary metaloxidesemiconductor (CMOS) technology without any design rule violations or process modifications. The optical receiver includes the Si APD bias controller that provides the Si APD reverse bias voltage by regulating the input dc voltage of the transimpedance amplifier (TIA) and the large negative output voltage of a voltage multiplier (VM) implemented in a separate chip. The bias controller determines the optimal reverse bias voltage by evaluating the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) of the received data. For the SNR evaluation, both the signal peak values and the peak-to-peak distances in the histogram generated by the asynchronous undersampling technique are used. It is experimentally verified that the monolithic optical receiver successfully achieves 25-Gb/s operation while automatically providing the Si APD with the reverse bias voltage that maximizes the SNR.

M. Eleraky, T. -Y. Huang and H. Wang, "A Compact, Wideband Complex Cascode Power Amplifier With LC Neutralization for VSWR-Resilient Operation in High-Density Phased Arrays," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2026.3654669.
Abstract: This article proposes a design methodology for achieving intrinsic performance resilience in power amplifiers (PAs) against antenna impedance variations inherent to modern phased-array systems through the improvement of the PA output matching ( $S\!_{22}$ ). This goal, however, presents a challenge for conventional cascode PAs due to their inherently high output impedance and often entails a direct tradeoff with PA output power and efficiency. To address this, a complex L-C neutralized cascode topology is developed to align the conjugate-match condition with the optimal load-pull impedance, thereby enabling a low output reflection coefficient without compromising the PA’s large-signal performance. In addition, the PA’s linearity is enhanced using a technique analogous to that of a multi-gate transistor (MGTR); in particular, the bias control is applied via the back gate of the SOI devices, leaving the primary RF signal path unperturbed. Abstract: This article presents a ring oscillator (RO)-based multiplying delay-locked loop (MDLL) that incorporates a dual-background calibration scheme to compensate for both injection phase and slew-rate mismatches. The MDLL employs the proposed frequency/slew-rate detector (FSD) to distinguish both mismatch types by comparing the pulse widths of consecutive output clock cycles before and after injection. X. Qiao et al., "MixCIM: A Hybrid Computing-in-Memory Macro With Less Data-Movement and Better Memory-Reuse for Depthwise Separable Neural Networks," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2026.3651775.
Abstract: Computing-in-memory (CIM) architectures have demonstrated strong potential for edge artificial intelligence (AI) devices due to their enhanced parallelism and energy efficiency. With the growing complexity of AI tasks and the rapid increase in model size, computation and deployment costs have surged. Depthwise separable neural networks (DSNNs) have attracted interest for offering appreciable performance with far fewer parameters. However, the core operation in DSNNs, depthwise separable convolution (DSC), exhibits a different multiply-and-accumulate (MAC) pattern that conventional CIM macros are not optimized to handle efficiently. As a result, deploying DSC on conventional CIM platforms remains challenging. In this article, we propose MixCIM, a mixed macro that integrates computing-near-memory (CNM) and CIM. It achieves high energy efficiency for DSC computations through a mix-memory computing architecture. With a 2’s complement storage-sign magnitude computation (TCS-SMC) encoding, it reduces the activations’ movement and MAC energy. A slidable eDRAM array enhances the parallelism of DW convolution to improve throughput. A memory sharing scheme between input and output activations increases effective memory use. Abstract: A 200-Gb/s 4-level pulse amplitude modulation (PAM-4) heterogeneous transceiver (TRX) for retimed pluggable optics is presented, which enables 200G transmission on the optical side and 100G transmission on the electrical side, without suffering from the exacerbated insertion loss (IL) of the electrical channel due to the doubled data rate from 100 to 200 Gb/s, and allows a much lower latency at similar power efficiency compared with the optical digital signal processor (ODSP) solution. Abstract: On-device learning at the edge enables low-latency, private personalization with improved long-term robustness and reduced maintenance costs. Y. -J. Lee, J. -G. Lee and H. -S. Kim, "A Fully Synthesizable, Fine-Grained Distributed Digital-LDO Fabric Featuring Freely Linkable Current-Sharing Network for Scalable On-Chip Power Delivery," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3648866.
Abstract: This article presents a fully synthesizable, fine-grained, distributed all-digital  $6\times 6$  LDO fabric in which modular micro-LDO regulator units are freely linkable—no centralized controller is required—enabling seamless scaling to arbitrarily large SoC power domains with minimal IR drop. The freely linkable current-sharing (FLCS) network exchanges only local digital output-current codes among adjacent LDOs, eliminating global routing bottlenecks and supporting various interconnection topologies (point-to-point, daisy-chain, tree, and hybrid) while ensuring balanced load sharing. Fabricated in 28-nm CMOS, each micro-LDO, synthesized using primitive digital cells, occupies only  $720~\mu $  m2 and delivers up to 25 mA, yielding a current density of 34.7 A/mm2. Y. O. Hassan, M. Oveisi, Z. Wang and P. Heydari, "An Antenna-to-Bits F-Band 120-Gbps CMOS RF-64QAM Receiver," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3648748.
Abstract: A CMOS 100–140-GHz end-to-end receiver (RX) is presented that integrates the antenna input all the way to the bitstream output, while demodulating 64QAM/16QAM/QPSK entirely in the analog domain. A sequential asynchronous demodulation method enables 120-Gbps operation at a notably small baseband power consumption. Fabricated in a 22-nm FDSOI CMOS process, the RX achieves a peak conversion gain of 32 dB and a minimum noise figure (NF) of 9.5 dB. E. Zijlma, M. S. Oude Alink, E. A. M. Klumperink and B. Nauta, "Wideband Simultaneous IM2 and IM3 Cancellation in FDSOI Resistive Step RF Attenuators," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3634465.
Abstract: This article presents a wideband resistive step RF attenuator implemented in 22-nm fully depleted silicon-on-insulator (FDSOI) CMOS technology with simultaneous second- and third-order intermodulation (IM2 and IM3) distortion cancellation. We propose a back-gate equalizer (BE) switch topology that converts even-order distortion into odd-order distortion, enabling simultaneous cancellation of the converted distortion and the third-order distortion at the attenuator output. This results in a highly linear and controllable attenuator. A five-step T-attenuator, including a bypass mode, is implemented, occupying 0.0066 mm2. An insertion loss (IL) of 1.7 dB in bypass mode is observed for both the BE and a reference (Ref.) design. Return loss (RL) remains better than 10 dB up to 10GHz for all settings, except in bypass mode, where it is better than 10 dB up to 7GHz. The measured IIP3 is approximately +30 dBm for both designs, while IIP2 ranges from +60 to +80 dBm for the BE design and around +50 dBm for the Ref. design. An improvement of up to 20 dB in IM2 is observed for the BE design compared to the Ref. design. Compared to prior work, this work also introduces a refined design strategy for IM3 distortion cancellation and several other circuit-level optimizations. The demonstrated IIP2 and IIP3 performance makes the attenuator designs well-suited for today’s high-linearity RF front-ends in highly integrated systems-on-chip (SoC).

A. Abdelaal, J. G. Kauffman, M. Dalla Longa, F. Conzatti and M. Ortmanns, "An 80 MHz CT DSM With SMASH DAC Achieving 106.2 dB SFDR Without Linearity Calibration," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3648344.
Abstract: Multi-bit quantization allows Delta-Sigma modulators to achieve low oversampling ratios (OSRs), improved jitter tolerance, and relaxed dynamic requirements of the loop filter, but face nonlinearity challenges in the feedback digital-to-analog converter (DAC), requiring complex calibration techniques. Dual quantization using digital DSM (DDSM) has been proposed to overcome this limitation, but state-of-the art DDSM suffers from limited resolution, stability concerns, and digital-analog matching requirements. This work applies a sturdy multistage noise shaping (SMASH) DDSM feedback DAC within a wideband DSM to address these issues. This results in low element mismatch sensitivity, eliminating the need for any DAC calibration. To reduce the effect of dynamic errors in the outer DAC, an inter-symbol-interference (ISI) shuffler is proposed and gain boosting is applied within the DAC elements to address memory effects due to parasitic capacitance. The DSM allows an excess loop-delay (ELD)  $= 1.5\times T_{s}$  in the outermost feedback path to give enough processing time for the SMASH DDSM. The prototype implementation of the 3rd-order continuous-time (CT) DSM shows a 106.2dB spurious-free dynamic range (SFDR) in an 80MHz bandwidth without any DAC calibration. Using reference folding and shuffling in the internal Flash quantizer, the DSM is clocked at 2.75 GHz. Implemented in 28 nm CMOS, CT DSM achieves a competitive figure-of-merit (FOM) = 163.7dB for such extreme linearity.

M. Anupam and I. Mondal, "A 2.7–15.5 GHz Programmable Bandwidth Continuous-Time Active Filter in 65 nm CMOS," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3643145.
Abstract: The article proposes and demonstrates bandwidth (BW) enhancement techniques for wideband and widely programmable active filters operating with a fixed and a single supply voltage. Fully differential transconductors having voltage-biased nMOS transistors form the input differential pair with a pair of pMOS transistors forming the active load. The gyrators’ common-mode (CM) response is kept under 0 dB at low frequencies by CM feedback (CMFB) loops. A pair of  ${\approx }300$  pH inductors and a capacitor, strategically shared among all transconductors, are used to sense and reject the high-frequency CM currents through the filter and provide CM stabilization at high frequencies. This extends the CM stability of the gyrators beyond the unity-gain bandwidth (UGB) of the CMFB loops. Another technique to relax the capacitance budget of the heavily loaded resistively terminated node of a leap-frog architecture is introduced. Background servo loops ensure automatic tracking and stabilization of the (trans) conductances across changes in ambient conditions. The efficacy of the proposed schemes is demonstrated through a singly terminated third-order Chebyshev low-pass filter implemented in a standard 65 nm bulk CMOS process operating with a fixed supply voltage of 1.1 V. The filter achieves a maximum −3 dB BW of 15.5 GHz, improving the state of the art by  ${\gt } 1.5{\times }$ . The filter’s BW is tunable from 2.7 to 15.5 GHz while faithfully preserving its response shape. Its measured signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) varies between 38 and 44 dB while consuming 31–291 mW of power between its minimum and maximum BW settings, respectively. Furthermore, the filter can also be reconfigured as a bandpass, with tunable center frequencies between 2.5 and 13.4 GHz.

Abstract: This article presents a power-efficient, voltage-controlled oscillator (VCO)-based wideband continuous-time multistage noise shaping (MASH) 3-1 delta–sigma modulator, designed for emerging high-data-rate wireless applications. Abstract: This article introduces a new technique for integrating a dense 2-D array of resonator-based pixels, enabling sub-wavelength-resolution near-field imaging. Y. Luo and N. Sun, "A Power-Efficient Jitter-Insensitive 3.2-GHz 1-bit CT ΔΣ Modulator With Direct Charge Dump Feedback," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3638779.
Abstract: Using a 1-bit quantizer (QTZ) in a wideband CT  $\Delta \Sigma $  modulator (DSM) is beneficial, but a 1-bit CT DSM imposes a stringent linearity requirement on OTA1, leading to high OTA1 power, and is sensitive to jitter when using a practical resistive/current-steering feedback. This work proposes to directly dump the feedback charge to the loop filter without the participation of OTA1, and OTA1 only processes the slow and small input signal and works for compensation, which does not require high accuracy, thus its power is significantly reduced. To efficiently reduce the non-common-mode noise from the biasing current provider in an OTA, a switched-capacitor (SC) supply is proposed. The modulator core consumes 4.2 mW, of which OTA1 only consumes 1.5 mW. The prototype achieves one of the best power efficiencies among the 1-bit, jitter-insensitive, and DAC-correction-free state-of-the-art CT  $\Delta \Sigma $  ADCs, validating the superiority of the proposed feedback in power consumption.

J. Yang et al., "A 33.6–136.2-TOPS/W Nonlinear Analog Computing-in-Memory Macro for Multi-Bit LSTM Accelerator in 65-nm CMOS," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3638445.
Abstract: The energy efficiency (EE) of analog computing-in-memory (ACIM) accelerator for recurrent neural networks (RNNs), particularly long short-term memory (LSTM) networks, is limited by the high proportion of nonlinear (NL) operations typically executed digitally. To address this, we propose an LSTM accelerator incorporating an ACIM macro with reconfigurable (1–5 bit) NL in-memory (NLIM) analog-to-digital converter (ADC) to compute NL activations directly in the analog domain using: 1) a dual-9T bitcell with decoupled read/write paths for signed inputs and ternary weight operations; 2) a read-word-line underdrive cascode (RUDC) technique achieving  $2.8\times $  higher read-bitline dynamic range (DR) than single-transistor designs ( $1.4\times $  better over conventional cascode structure with  $7\times $  lower current variation); and 3) a dual-supply 6T-static random access memory (SRAM) array for efficient multi-bit weight operations and reducing both bitcell count ( $7.8\times $ ) and latency ( $4\times $ ) for 5-bit weight operations. We experimentally demonstrate a 5-bit NLIM ADC for approximating NL activations in LSTM cells, achieving average error <1 LSB. Simulation confirms the robustness of NLIM ADC against temperature variations thanks to the replica bias strategy. Our design achieves 92.0% on-chip inference accuracy for a 12-class keyword-spotting (KWS) task while demonstrating  $2.2\times $  higher system-level normalized EE and  $1.6\times $  better normalized area efficiency than state-of-the-art (SOTA) works. The results combine physical measurements of a macro unit—accounting for the majority of LSTM operations (99% linear and 80% NL operations)—with simulations of the remaining components, including additional LSTM and fully connected (FC) layers.

Y. Zhang, N. Zhu, F. Meng, K. Wang, K. Ma and K. S. Yeo, "Design and Analysis of a Compact CMOS SOI Power Amplifier With 29.2-dBm P SAT and 44.4% PAE for FR3 5G-Advanced/6G Beamforming Systems," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3639947.
Abstract: This article presents a compact, high-efficiency power amplifier (PA) for frequency range 3 (FR3) beamformer integrated circuits (BFICs) targeting 5G-Advanced and 6G systems. To address the output power, efficiency, and integration challenges of silicon-based PAs in high-density antenna arrays, an extended cascode core (ECC) leveraging floating-body (FB) silicon-on-insulator (SOI) transistors is adopted to provide improved power handling with a stable breakdown margin, and RC-feedback networks that further enhance broadband stability and output matching. A triple-coupled 4-to-1 folded transformer (FT) with odd-/even-mode co-optimization enables efficient power combining, incorporating an embedded  $\pi $ -network that suppresses the 2nd harmonic ( $2{f_{0}}$ ) to sustain high output power and efficiency in a compact footprint. Fabricated in the 0.13- $\mu $ m CMOS SOI technology, the PA achieves a 3-dB bandwidth of  $9.0{-}13.5$  GHz, 29.2dBm saturated output power ( $P_{\text {SAT}}$ ), and 44.4% peak power-added efficiency (PAE). The core area is 0.697mm2, corresponding to a 1193.3 mW/mm2 power density (PD). It supports 64-QAM modulation at an average power of 22.6dBm with an error vector magnitude (EVM)/adjacent channel leakage ratio (ACLR) of −25dB and −31dBc, respectively. The PA exhibits amplitude-to-phase (AM–PM) distortion below 2.5°/2.1°/1.8° at 10/11/12GHz up to 1-dB compression point ( $P_{\text {1 dB}}$ ), and  ${\lt } {-}36$  dBc 2nd-/3rd-harmonic suppression across the band. The PA achieves state-of-the-art PAE and PD among FR3-band silicon-based PAs with competitive  $P_{\text {SAT}}$ , offering an integration-friendly, energy-efficient solution for massive multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO) transmitters in next-generation wireless systems.

X. Cai et al., "A Constant Bandwidth Oversampling PLL Enabled by a Constant-Gain Oversampler Composed of Double-Edge and Sampling Single-Edge Capacitive Charging and Discharging Phase Interpolators," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3640681.
Abstract: This work introduces a constant bandwidth oversampling PLL (CBW-OSPLL) that eliminates the noise degradation and stability limitations of conventional oversampling phase-locked loops (PLLs) caused by sampling gain fluctuations. The proposed CBW-OSPLL utilizes a constant-gain oversampler composed of double-edge capacitive charging and discharging PIs (DE-CCDPIs) and sampling single-edge capacitive charging and discharging PIs (SSE-CCDPIs) with embedded reference sampling functionality. The DE-CCDPIs provide eight clocks with phases uniformly distributed within a reference cycle, which are further evenly split into 16 phases through SSE-CCDPIs. The intrinsic sampling functionality embedded in the proposed SSE-CCDPI ensures a constant sampling across all sampling locations, enabling improved noise and stability. To further improve the linearity of the DE-CCDPIs and SSE-CCDPIs, direct-gating transition and voltage clamping techniques are proposed, collectively reducing the differential nonlinearity of the DE-CCDPIs and SSE-CCDPIs to 0.001 LSB. Fabricated in a standard 0.18- $\mu $ m CMOS, the proposed integer-N CBW-OSPLL occupies an active area of 0.44 mm2. When operating at 2 GHz, it achieves an rms jitter of 444 fs and consumes 7.6mW, yielding an FoMJIT of −238.2 dB and an FoMREF of −240.2dB, while measurements across supply variation, temperature variation, and multiple samples are performed, demonstrating the robustness of the proposed CBW-OSPLL.

Y. Wang et al., "A 77.35 TOPS/W Transformer Processor Exploiting Multi-Scope Similarity in Attention Computing," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3640176.
Abstract: Transformer-based models have achieved remarkable success in several artificial intelligence (AI) fields. Their superior performance relies on the global attention mechanism, which computes the relevance of any two input tokens to reach a global-level receptive field. Generally, the input of transformer-based models contains similar content due to natural redundancy in human language or images. Similar input contents generate similar vectors during global attention computation, which contain substantial identical or similar operands. These similar vectors introduce vector-wise redundancy, element-wise duplication, and inefficient bit-wise transitions in global attention computing. This article proposes an energy-efficient transformer processor that exploits dynamic similarity in global attention computing. First, a principal-component-prior speculation deduces the quantization region of each output pixel during matrix multiplication (MM) to terminate the computation early, which omits vector-wise redundancy. Second, a similar-vector tracked computing engine dynamically tracks and gathers similar vectors for parallel computing, which reuses the multiplication results from duplicated operands for element-wise computations reduction. Third, a bit-wise stationary processing element (BSPE) reorders the computation to minimize partial product (PP) transition counts among similar operands, reducing bit-wise logic transition energy. Fabricated in 28-nm CMOS technology, the proposed processor occupies an area of 6.4 mm2. It reaches 77.35 TOPS/W peak energy efficiency when performing ImageNet classification on the vision transformer base (ViT-B) model. Compared with the state-of-the-art transformer processor, the proposed processor reduces energy by  $3.69\times $  and offers  $4.13\times $  speedup.
keywords: {Vectors;Transformers;Computational modeling;Energy efficiency;Parallel processing;Artificial intelligence;Redundancy;Logic;Computer architecture;Technological innovation;Duplication reusing;out-of-order computing;processor;self-attention;similarity speculating;transformer},

J. Wan, Y. Shen, Y. Ding, S. Xu, Y. Qian and S. Hu, "A 145-GHz Self-Packaged Outphasing Transmitter Achieving 39-dBm EIRP at 11-pJ/b/m in 40-nm CMOS," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3639016.
Abstract: This article presents a highly integrated, high-power outphasing transmitter with self-packaging technique in the D band. The transmitter achieves system-level optimization through the co-design of complementary metal–oxide –semiconductor (CMOS) active circuits, on-chip antennas, and off-chip packaged. By leveraging ultra-low-profile source-integrated metasurface technology, the design transforms an air-fed lens antenna into a cost-effective multilayer printed circuit board (PCB), effectively reducing the packaging height from one focal length to one-fifth, corresponding to an 80% reduction. This structural optimization significantly enhances the equivalent isotropic radiated power (EIRP) while maintaining power consumption, thereby realizing self-packaged. Furthermore, the concept of in-antenna power combining is extended to an outphasing power-combining network, facilitating the realization of a fully integrated D-band outphasing transmitter chip. This design ensures high fidelity and high output power even when transmitting advanced modulation signals. Measurement results indicate that this self-packaged outphasing transmitter achieves an EIRP of 39 dBm at 145 GHz with a power consumption of 924 mW, and a 3-dB EIRP bandwidth from 130 to 156 GHz, covering 18.2% of the frequency range. To the best of the authors’ knowledge, this D-band CMOS self-packaged outphasing transmitter achieves the highest EIRP.

J. Oh, C. So, H. Kim, S. Hong and S. Cho, "A Fast-Settling mm-Wave LO With I/Q-Calibrated SSB Mixer and Frequency-Tuned ILO Filter Achieving Sub-ns Settling Time and −56 dBc Spur," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3634591.
Abstract: This article presents a fast-settling 60-GHz local oscillator (LO) for stepped-carrier orthogonal frequency-division multiplexing (OFDM), employing an I/Q-calibrated single-sideband (SSB) mixer with a frequency-tuned injection-locked oscillator (ILO) filter. The SSB mixer enables instantaneous frequency hopping, while the ILO acts as a high- $Q$  bandpass filter that suppresses mixer-induced spurs. To ensure robust injection locking and maximize spur suppression, the ILO’s self-resonant frequency is tightly aligned with the target frequency via a background frequency-tuning loop (FTL). Background I/Q calibrations for the SSB mixer inputs are implemented to further reduce spurs. In addition, a capacitance-neutralized buffer mitigates amplitude-to-frequency modulation (AM–FM) conversion in the millimeter-wave (mm-Wave) voltage-controlled oscillator (VCO), leading to improved phase noise. Fabricated in 28-nm CMOS, the prototype achieves a sub-ns settling time, a worst case spur of −56 dBc, and 60.3 fs rms jitter while consuming 73.1 mW.

J. -H. Kim et al., "A Real-Time Link-Adaptive 3-D Resonant Current-Mode Wireless Receiver With Self-Optimizing Control," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3637095.
Abstract: This article presents a 3-D resonant current-mode wireless power receiver (RCM 3D-Rx) with autonomous optimum resonance cycle ( $N_{\mathrm {OPT}}$ ) detection and real-time link adaptivity for implantable medical devices (IMDs). The proposed design integrates three orthogonally oriented receiver coils within a com pact 3-D-printed PA12 structure, enabling orientation-insensitive power transfer without external programming or lookup tables (LUTs). Each receiver axis independently determines and periodically re-evaluates its  $N_{\mathrm {OPT}}$  to adapt to real-time coupling variations, while a non-residual  $V_{\mathrm {Rx}}$  feedback loop (NRFL) ensures complete energy transfer from the  $LC$  tank, improving output power by up to 38.0%. M. Shi et al., "SparseCol: A 1320 BTOPS/W Precision-Scalable NPU Exploiting Training-Free Structured Bit-Level Sparsity and Dynamic Dataflow," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3636451.
Abstract: Bit-serial computation enables sequential processing of data at the bit level, providing several advantages, such as scalable computational precision. This approach has gained significant attention, especially for exploiting bit-level sparsity (BLS) in AI workloads. While current bit-serial processors leverage BLS to eliminate the computation associated with zero bits, they face a fundamental tradeoff: either they suffer from low memory-access and computation efficiency caused by irregular patterns of non-zero bits or they incur substantial area overhead from complex online scheduling mechanisms required to reorganize bit-level data and preserve memory access and computation regularity. Therefore, we present the SparseCol processor, designed to harness extensive bit sparsity while maintaining high hardware utilization across various AI applications, including convolutional neural networks (CNNs), RNNs, and Transformers. In contrast to traditional methods, SparseCol exploits structured BLS, denoted by bit-column sparsity, without requiring any re-training. Furthermore, SparseCol implements a dynamic dataflow (DF) architecture that tackles hardware under-utilization issues commonly found in existing bit-serial solutions. Fabricated in 16-nm CMOS node, SparseCol delivers 1320 BTOPS/W (BTOPS represents binary tera-operations per second, calculated as  $\#\text {Wbits} \times \#\text {Abits} \times \text {TOPS}$ ) peak efficiency while maintaining accuracy, outperforming SoTA sparse processors in terms of efficiency by  $6.8{\times }$ . Comprehensive evaluations on CNN classification tasks and Transformer architectures demonstrate system-level efficiencies of 745.02 BTOPS/W and 850.5 BTOPS/W, respectively.

J. Lu, J. Sun, B. Li, Q. Zhuang, Y. Shi and H. Qiu, "A Galvanic Isolator Achieving 117-Mb/s Forward Data Transfer in the Presence of 181-kV/μs Common-Mode Transient Interference," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3635266.
Abstract: Targeting a high data rate (DR) in the presence of common-mode transient (CMT) interference in the isolated gate driver application scenario, we presented an inductively galvanic isolator (GI) consisting of transmitter (TX) and receiver (RX) dies. To eliminate the destructive effects of CMT interference, an adaptive transconductance enhancement (ATE) technique was proposed on the TX side, which enables real-time detection of CMT events and contributes to an enhanced  $g_{m}$  to stabilize the oscillator’s operation. For the data transfer, we observed the problem of unequal data symbol durations using the conventional frequency-shift keying (FSK) demodulator, which can lead to shoot-through current or large reverse conduction loss. This problem was solved by the proposed compensated FSK demodulator. Both TX and RX dies in the proposed GI were fabricated in a 0.18- $\mu $ m BCD process. Experimental results verified that the proposed GI demonstrates static CMT immunity (CMTI) up to 299 kV/ $\mu $ s. Furthermore, even in the presence of a severe CMT interference of 181 kV/ $\mu $ s, the GI supports a high DR of 117 Mb/s, corresponding to a DR ratio (DRC) of 67%, with a low bit error rate (BER) of  $10^{-8}$ . This extends the state of the art by  $3.72\times $  in DRC and  $1.13\times$  in dynamic CMTI.

P. Deaville, B. Zhang and N. Verma, "Analyzing Embedded Non-Volatile Memory and Technology Optimizations for In-Memory Computing," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3632212.
Abstract: This article quantitatively analyzes the limitations to energy efficiency and compute density for in-memory computing (IMC) based on today’s embedded non-volatile memory (eNVM) technology and provides insights for future technology optimizations for IMC application. While eNVM technologies have drawn interest for IMC due to their recent integration in foundry CMOS technologies and their ability to achieve high memory densities, IMC operation derives its efficiency and throughput density advantages through a signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) tradeoff. This article shows that today’s CMOS-integrated eNVM presents technology parameters that make IMC SNR limited by the readout circuitry, substantially preventing the benefits of memory density to be exploited, and limiting eNVM-based IMC below the efficiency and throughput density of prevailing digital accelerators. This article develops parametric models for IMC energy based on the fundamental tradeoffs and evaluates practical demonstrations of a recently presented magnetoresistive random access memory (MRAM)-based IMC design and a newly presented resistive RAM (ReRAM)-based IMC design against this. This can help guide future technology optimization targets for eNVM in IMC applications.

F. Chen, Q. Pan and C. P. Yue, "A 56-Gbaud 7.3-V ppd Linear Modulator Transmitter With AMUX-Based Inherent FFE and Breakdown Voltage Tripler in 130-nm SiGe BiCMOS," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3631957.
Abstract: This article presents a half-rate linear transmitter monolithically integrating a 2-to-1 analog multiplexer (AMUX) and a linear driver for optical modulators. Using the timing relationship between the clock signal and half-rate data streams, the AMUX achieves an inherent feed-forward equalizer (FFE), which can be re-configured into two-tap or three-tap mode by adjusting the clock delay. Additionally, to improve the output voltage swing and linearity, a topology named breakdown voltage (BV) tripler is proposed for the linear driver. By stacking three heterogeneous bipolar transistors (HBTs) and biasing the bases of the top two HBTs using amplified input signals, the driver achieves an output swing three times larger than that of conventional cascode topology while maintaining good reliability. Fabricated in 130-nm SiGe BiCMOS, the proposed linear driver achieves a dc gain of 17.1 dB, a 6-dB bandwidth of 39.1 GHz and a total harmonic distortion (THD) of 1.6% at 6- ${V_{\mathrm {ppd}}}~1$ -GHz sinusoidal output. The complete transmitter (AMUX + driver) achieves a maximum output swing of  $7.3~{V_{\mathrm {ppd}}}$  at 56-Gb/s non-return-to-zero (NRZ), as well as a maximum data rate of 112-Gb/s PAM-4 with a swing of  $4.2~{V_{\mathrm {ppd}}}$  when the inherent FFE is enabled.

C. Ding, M. Gao, A. K. Skrivervik and M. Shoaran, "A 3-mm2, Multi-Data-Rate, Low-Power IR-UWB Transmitter With On-Chip Antenna for Distributed, Free-Floating Neural Implants," in IEEE Journal of Solid-State Circuits, doi: 10.1109/JSSC.2025.3631653.
Abstract: Distributed, high-density neural implants enable multi-region, high-fidelity neural recording and targeted stimulation, advancing both the treatment of neurological disorders and the development of brain–machine interfaces. While recent uplink transmitters (TXs) have achieved high data rates, high energy efficiency, and mm-scale footprints suitable for distributed systems, they struggle to maintain a balanced performance across these metrics. This article introduces a mm-scale wireless TX to overcome this limitation. To support high-density neural recording with flexible channel counts and various signal modalities, the TX offers energy-efficient data transmission from 50 Mb/s to 1.2 Gb/s, enabled by a novel dual-modulation technique. At high data rates, the TX employs a hybrid modulation scheme combining 4-PPM and 2-PSK, while at low data rates, it switches to simple on-off keying (OOK) modulation. To enable this modulation strategy, we introduce a twin-oscillator-based wideband phase-locked loop (PLL) for RF carrier generation within the ultra-wideband (UWB) range. At high data rates, the PLL directly generates a low-jitter UWB carrier. At low data rates, leveraging OOK modulation’s relaxed phase noise requirements, a duty-cycled  $5{\times }$  frequency multiplier upconverts the PLL’s low output frequency to the UWB band. This approach bypasses the power-hungry RF oscillators and dividers, facilitating energy-efficient, duty-cycled carrier generation and supporting frequency-division multiple access (FDMA). In addition, we employ a hybrid strategy combining time-division multiple access (TDMA) and FDMA to enhance scalability for untethered, distributed neural implants. A custom on-chip antenna replaces the conventional printed circuit board (PCB) antenna, establishing a wireless link between the implant and the external system. The proposed TX, fabricated in 65-nm CMOS, occupies a compact footprint of only 3 mm2 and consumes 1.49–7.26 mW across all supported data rates (50–1200 Mb/s). In in vitro wireless testing, it achieved a record transcutaneous transmission range of 60 cm through 15-mm thick pork tissue, without the need for a subcranial repeater.
